Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. Um, is this chair okay? Great. 

Well, I guess what brings me in is... I've been having a little bit of trouble just, you know, staying in my seat lately. It's really not a huge deal, I'm just an active person and I like to stretch my legs! But... I suppose it's getting to the point where I keep getting up when I'm really supposed to just stay put. 

Like, I'll be right in the middle of client interviews—I'm a private investigator, by the way—or during tutoring sessions, or even just sitting with my book club. I just sort of pop up. Well, I guess... I mean, I try to! I really do enjoy quiet time. But lately, things that used to be relaxing for me, like, um, yoga or just sitting and reading with my book club... they haven't been quite as easy. 

It's nothing terrible! I just... I find myself whispering little comments to the person next to me, or maybe I'm rustling my papers a bit too much. Or I'll just suddenly think of a reason I need to get up and grab something instead of just staying still and focusing. 

I know it probably sounds a little disruptive, and I feel so bad if I bother anyone else trying to enjoy the quiet. I'm really trying to be better about it. Do you think that's something a lot of people struggle with as they get a little older? I hope it's not too unusual.

Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener.

Is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: When you get up from your seat during these situations, are you feeling restless or like you need to move?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't really say restless. I mean, maybe a tiny bit? But it's more just... I have a lot of energy, you know? I'm a very energetic person. \n\nI don't feel like I *have* to move, it's just that sitting still for a long time can feel a little... tedious, I guess? So I just stretch my legs. It's not like I'm pacing around in a panic or anything like that. I just like to be on my feet. \n\nIs that normal? I mean, a lot of people like to stand up and stretch, right? I'm not doing anything too weird, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to engage in quiet activities like reading or playing a quiet game?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I try to! I really do enjoy quiet time. But lately, things that used to be relaxing for me, like, um, yoga or just sitting and reading with my book club... they haven't been quite as easy. \n\nIt's nothing terrible! I just... I find myself whispering little comments to the person next to me, or maybe I'm rustling my papers a bit too much. Or I'll just suddenly think of a reason I need to get up and grab something instead of just staying still and focusing. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little disruptive, and I feel so bad if I bother anyone else trying to enjoy the quiet. I'm really trying to be better about it. Do you think that's something a lot of people struggle with as they get a little older? I hope it's not too unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in line or in conversations?\n\nPatient: Oh, waiting my turn? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm a very patient person, really! I always try to be polite. \n\nI guess... sometimes in conversations, I might get a little excited and jump in, but just because I'm so engaged in what the other person is saying! I never mean to interrupt. And waiting in lines... well, nobody really likes waiting in lines, right? I might tap my foot or look around a bit, but I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I just wait like everyone else. \n\nThat's a pretty normal way to be, isn't it? I just try to keep things moving along.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or keeping track of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing my tasks? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that at all. Actually, I take a lot of pride in being really on top of things! In my line of work—I'm a private investigator—you really have to keep track of every little detail and watch the clock closely, or things can get messy pretty fast. \n\nSo I always make sure I have my planners and my lists. I mean, sure, occasionally a day might get a little away from me if I'm busy, but that happens to absolutely everyone, right? I try really, really hard to be reliable and punctual for my clients. That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener. \n\nSometimes, if I know exactly what someone is going to ask—especially with my clients, you know, because I've been doing this for a while—I might just sort of... help them finish their sentence? Just to show I'm following along and to be helpful! I always want people to feel like I understand them. But I definitely don't just shout things out rudely. I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. 

I guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. 

But it's only because I want to be helpful! I just get so enthusiastic about solving things, or I want to make sure they aren't missing a detail. I mean, afterward, I do sometimes realize, "Oh, I just barged right into their conversation," and I feel a little bad about it. But I always apologize if I feel like I stepped on any toes! 

That's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope people don't think I'm being pushy or intrusive. I'd hate for you to think I don't have good social skills!

Saying things I regret? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that very often. I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.

I mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. 

If I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. 

Everyone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? I really do try my best to be thoughtful about what I say.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Trouble controlling impulses? Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I really value being a polite and agreeable person, you know? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. 

I mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. 

I really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.

I always try to be polite. \n\nI guess... sometimes in conversations, I might get a little excited and jump in, but just because I'm so engaged in what the other person is saying! I never mean to interrupt. And waiting in lines... well, nobody really likes waiting in lines, right? I might tap my foot or look around a bit, but I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I just wait like everyone else. \n\nThat's a pretty normal way to be, isn't it? I just try to keep things moving along.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or keeping track of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing my tasks? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that at all. Actually, I take a lot of pride in being really on top of things! In my line of work—I'm a private investigator—you really have to keep track of every little detail and watch the clock closely, or things can get messy pretty fast. \n\nSo I always make sure I have my planners and my lists. I mean, sure, occasionally a day might get a little away from me if I'm busy, but that happens to absolutely everyone, right? I try really, really hard to be reliable and punctual for my clients. That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener. \n\nSometimes, if I know exactly what someone is going to ask—especially with my clients, you know, because I've been doing this for a while—I might just sort of... help them finish their sentence? Just to show I'm following along and to be helpful! I always want people to feel like I understand them. But I definitely don't just shout things out rudely. I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on one thing for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.\n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes if I'm doing paperwork or something really quiet, I might... you know, take a few little breaks? Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful! I just get so enthusiastic about solving things, or I want to make sure they aren't missing a detail. I mean, afterward, I do sometimes realize, \"Oh, I just barged right into their conversation,\" and I feel a little bad about it. But I always apologize if I feel like I stepped on any toes! \n\nThat's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope people don't think I'm being pushy or intrusive. I'd hate for you to think I don't have good social skills!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself saying things that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Saying things I regret? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that very often. I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! 

I mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? In my line of work—I'm a private investigator—you really have to keep track of every little detail and watch the clock closely, or things can get messy pretty fast. \n\nSo I always make sure I have my planners and my lists. I mean, sure, occasionally a day might get a little away from me if I'm busy, but that happens to absolutely everyone, right? I try really, really hard to be reliable and punctual for my clients. That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener. \n\nSometimes, if I know exactly what someone is going to ask—especially with my clients, you know, because I've been doing this for a while—I might just sort of... help them finish their sentence? Just to show I'm following along and to be helpful! I always want people to feel like I understand them. But I definitely don't just shout things out rudely. I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on one thing for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.\n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes if I'm doing paperwork or something really quiet, I might... you know, take a few little breaks? Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. It's nothing reckless or out of control. I really pride myself on being responsible, even when no one is watching. \n\nI don't really get urges to do anything strange or impulsive when I'm on my own. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! \n\nI mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? I have to notice the little things—who just walked into the room, what kind of car is idling across the street, that sort of thing. I really try my best to stay on top of everything and do a good job.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking, even if you don't mean to?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 49 / private_detective_or_investigator / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Interrupting_Intruding\nmanifestation: \"If I hear junior detectives discussing a lead or a neighbor talking in the hallway, I jump in with questions or advice before I've been asked, and afterward I realize I barged into someone else's conversation.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Interrupting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I interrupt people. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. Being polite and respectful of others is just so important to me. 

I mean... I suppose sometimes I can be a little *too* eager to help? Like, if I happen to hear the junior detectives at the agency discussing a lead, or maybe even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway, I might sort of... jump in. Just to offer some advice or ask a few questions before they've actually invited me into the chat. 

But it's only because I want to be helpful and share my experience! I just get so invested in solving problems and making sure people have the right information. Though, I will admit, sometimes afterward I'll look back and realize that I kind of barged into someone else's conversation. I always feel terrible when I realize I've done that, because I'd hate for anyone to think I'm rude or overbearing. I really do mean well. That's just being a collaborative team player, right? I really hope they see it that way.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Talk more than others? Oh, um... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything like that. I really try to be engaging and make sure the people I'm with feel comfortable. I think communication is just so vital, especially in my line of work.

I mean, I do like to be thorough. Sometimes, if I'm discussing a case or going over a client meeting, I... well, I guess I can get a little caught up in the details. I just want to make sure I'm explaining every single angle so nothing is missed. I'll be talking through all the possibilities, and sometimes I'll realize I've sort of... filled up all the space in the conversation. Even when the other person is maybe shifting around or looking at their watch, giving me those little signs that they're ready to move on. 

But I'm just trying to be comprehensive and make sure they have all the facts! It's so important to be clear, don't you think? I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just rambling or not letting them speak. I really just want to do a good job and make sure everything is understood. That's a good quality for an investigator, isn't it?

I just wait like everyone else. \n\nThat's a pretty normal way to be, isn't it? I just try to keep things moving along.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or keeping track of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing my tasks? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that at all. Actually, I take a lot of pride in being really on top of things! In my line of work—I'm a private investigator—you really have to keep track of every little detail and watch the clock closely, or things can get messy pretty fast. \n\nSo I always make sure I have my planners and my lists. I mean, sure, occasionally a day might get a little away from me if I'm busy, but that happens to absolutely everyone, right? I try really, really hard to be reliable and punctual for my clients. That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener. \n\nSometimes, if I know exactly what someone is going to ask—especially with my clients, you know, because I've been doing this for a while—I might just sort of... help them finish their sentence? Just to show I'm following along and to be helpful! I always want people to feel like I understand them. But I definitely don't just shout things out rudely. I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on one thing for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.\n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes if I'm doing paperwork or something really quiet, I might... you know, take a few little breaks? Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful! I just get so enthusiastic about solving things, or I want to make sure they aren't missing a detail. I mean, afterward, I do sometimes realize, \"Oh, I just barged right into their conversation,\" and I feel a little bad about it. But I always apologize if I feel like I stepped on any toes! \n\nThat's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope people don't think I'm being pushy or intrusive. I'd hate for you to think I don't have good social skills!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself saying things that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Saying things I regret? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that very often. I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful! I just get so enthusiastic about solving things, or I want to make sure they aren't missing a detail. I mean, afterward, I do sometimes realize, \"Oh, I just barged right into their conversation,\" and I feel a little bad about it. But I always apologize if I feel like I stepped on any toes! \n\nThat's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope people don't think I'm being pushy or intrusive. I'd hate for you to think I don't have good social skills!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself saying things that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Saying things I regret? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that very often. I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? I really do try my best to be thoughtful about what I say.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble controlling your impulses in social interactions?\n\nPatient: Trouble controlling impulses? Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I really value being a polite and agreeable person, you know? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Fidgeting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I fidget. I really try to sit nicely and maintain a professional posture. It's so important to look put-together, especially when I'm working on a case. 

I mean... I suppose when I'm stuck at the computer for hours, just combing through endless public records or financial statements, I might... well, I guess my foot might start bouncing under the desk a little bit. Or I might find myself clicking a pen without really thinking about it. And sometimes I do shift around in my chair, maybe a bit more than I should, to the point where I actually lose my place in what I'm reading. 

But it's just because I'm trying to stay alert and focused on the task! Those records can be so dry, and I just want to make sure I'm keeping my energy up so I don't miss any important details for the client. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just restless or not taking the work seriously. I really do try to stay on task. That's just a normal way to keep the mind sharp during tedious work, isn't it? I really hope it doesn't sound like a bad habit.

I just wait like everyone else. \n\nThat's a pretty normal way to be, isn't it? I just try to keep things moving along.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or keeping track of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing my tasks? That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on one thing for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.\n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes if I'm doing paperwork or something really quiet, I might... you know, take a few little breaks? Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful! Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. When I'm by myself, I'm actually pretty quiet. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? I really do try my best to be thoughtful about what I say.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble controlling your impulses in social interactions?\n\nPatient: Trouble controlling impulses? Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I really value being a polite and agreeable person, you know? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. When I'm by myself, I'm actually pretty quiet. I mostly just focus on my case files, or maybe catch up on some paperwork for the agency. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes I might suddenly decide to deep-clean my office or start looking into a background check I hadn't planned on doing right that second, but... well, that's just being dedicated to the job, right? It's nothing reckless or out of control. I really pride myself on being responsible, even when no one is watching. \n\nI don't really get urges to do anything strange or impulsive when I'm on my own. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! \n\nI mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? I have to notice the little things—who just walked into the room, what kind of car is idling across the street, that sort of thing. So if it ever seems like I'm looking around a lot or noticing things outside the window, it's really just me being thorough and observant. \n\nI'd never let myself get pulled away from what I'm supposed to be doing. I take my responsibilities very seriously. Being highly observant is a good trait to have, isn't it? I really try my best to stay on top of everything and do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking, even if you don't mean to?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I interrupt people. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. Being polite and respectful of others is just so important to me. \n\nI mean... I suppose sometimes I can be a little *too* eager to help? Like, if I happen to hear the junior detectives at the agency discussing a lead, or maybe even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway, I might sort of... jump in. Just to offer some advice or ask a few questions before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful and share my experience! I just get so invested in solving problems and making sure people have the right information. Though, I will admit, sometimes afterward I'll look back and realize that I kind of barged into someone else's conversation. I always feel terrible when I realize I've done that, because I'd hate for anyone to think I'm rude or overbearing. I really do mean well. That's just being a collaborative team player, right? I really hope they see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talk more than others? Oh, um... Speaking without thinking? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that. I really pride myself on being thoughtful and professional when I speak. In my line of work, you have to be very careful about what you say, especially when interviewing witnesses or talking to clients. I always try to be very intentional.

Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? I really do try my best to be thoughtful about what I say.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble controlling your impulses in social interactions?\n\nPatient: Trouble controlling impulses? Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I really value being a polite and agreeable person, you know? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. When I'm by myself, I'm actually pretty quiet. I mostly just focus on my case files, or maybe catch up on some paperwork for the agency. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes I might suddenly decide to deep-clean my office or start looking into a background check I hadn't planned on doing right that second, but... well, that's just being dedicated to the job, right? It's nothing reckless or out of control. I really pride myself on being responsible, even when no one is watching. \n\nI don't really get urges to do anything strange or impulsive when I'm on my own. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! \n\nI mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? I have to notice the little things—who just walked into the room, what kind of car is idling across the street, that sort of thing. So if it ever seems like I'm looking around a lot or noticing things outside the window, it's really just me being thorough and observant. \n\nI'd never let myself get pulled away from what I'm supposed to be doing. I take my responsibilities very seriously. Being highly observant is a good trait to have, isn't it? I really try my best to stay on top of everything and do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking, even if you don't mean to?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I interrupt people. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. Being polite and respectful of others is just so important to me. \n\nI mean... I suppose sometimes I can be a little *too* eager to help? Like, if I happen to hear the junior detectives at the agency discussing a lead, or maybe even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway, I might sort of... jump in. Just to offer some advice or ask a few questions before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful and share my experience! I just get so invested in solving problems and making sure people have the right information. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Careless mistakes? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really pride myself on being very precise. When you're an investigator, you just have to be. I try so hard to make sure every single detail in my reports is perfect.

It's so important to be reliable like that, don't you agree? I'd be absolutely mortified if I handed in something with errors. I really hope I don't seem like someone who doesn't pay attention to the details, because I truly do try my absolute best to get everything exactly right.

I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I've been having a little bit of trouble just, you know, staying in my seat lately. It's really not a huge deal, I'm just an active person and I like to stretch my legs! But... I suppose it's getting to the point where I keep getting up when I'm really supposed to just stay put. \n\nLike, I'll be right in the middle of client interviews—I'm a private investigator, by the way—or during tutoring sessions, or even just sitting with my book club. I just sort of pop up. And, um, people are starting to notice that I can't just sit and listen. I feel awful because I don't want anyone to think I'm being rude or ignoring them, which I definitely don't mean to be! \n\nDoes that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: When you get up from your seat during these situations, are you feeling restless or like you need to move?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't really say restless. I mean, maybe a tiny bit? But it's more just... I have a lot of energy, you know? I'm a very energetic person. \n\nI don't feel like I *have* to move, it's just that sitting still for a long time can feel a little... tedious, I guess? So I just stretch my legs. It's not like I'm pacing around in a panic or anything like that. I just like to be on my feet. \n\nIs that normal? I mean, a lot of people like to stand up and stretch, right? I'm not doing anything too weird, am I?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to engage in quiet activities like reading or playing a quiet game?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I try to! I really do enjoy quiet time. But lately, things that used to be relaxing for me, like, um, yoga or just sitting and reading with my book club... they haven't been quite as easy. \n\nIt's nothing terrible! I just... I find myself whispering little comments to the person next to me, or maybe I'm rustling my papers a bit too much. Or I'll just suddenly think of a reason I need to get up and grab something instead of just staying still and focusing. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little disruptive, and I feel so bad if I bother anyone else trying to enjoy the quiet. I'm really trying to be better about it. Do you think that's something a lot of people struggle with as they get a little older? I hope it's not too unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in line or in conversations?\n\nPatient: Oh, waiting my turn? Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm a very patient person, really! I always try to be polite. \n\nI guess... sometimes in conversations, I might get a little excited and jump in, but just because I'm so engaged in what the other person is saying! I never mean to interrupt. And waiting in lines... well, nobody really likes waiting in lines, right? I might tap my foot or look around a bit, but I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I just wait like everyone else. \n\nThat's a pretty normal way to be, isn't it? I just try to keep things moving along.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or keeping track of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, organizing my tasks? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that at all. Actually, I take a lot of pride in being really on top of things! In my line of work—I'm a private investigator—you really have to keep track of every little detail and watch the clock closely, or things can get messy pretty fast. \n\nSo I always make sure I have my planners and my lists. I mean, sure, occasionally a day might get a little away from me if I'm busy, but that happens to absolutely everyone, right? I try really, really hard to be reliable and punctual for my clients. That's a good sign, isn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm just completely all over the place!\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Oh, blurting things out? Um... no, I don't think I really do that. I mean, I try very hard to be a good listener. \n\nSometimes, if I know exactly what someone is going to ask—especially with my clients, you know, because I've been doing this for a while—I might just sort of... help them finish their sentence? Just to show I'm following along and to be helpful! I always want people to feel like I understand them. But I definitely don't just shout things out rudely. I'd be so embarrassed if I made someone feel like I wasn't listening to them. \n\nThat's just being engaged in the conversation, right? I really hope it doesn't come across as impolite.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus on one thing for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, focusing for a long time? Um, well, I think I'm pretty good at it, really! In my job, you have to be able to sit and watch a location for hours, and I do that all the time. I'm very dedicated to my work.\n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes if I'm doing paperwork or something really quiet, I might... you know, take a few little breaks? Just to stretch my legs or grab a coffee. But that's just to keep my energy up so I can do a better job when I sit back down! I wouldn't say it's *hard* to focus. I just like to stay fresh. \n\nEveryone needs a little break now and then to stay sharp, don't they? I really try my best to stay on task.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, um... well, I really try not to be rude. I value good manners so much, and I always want people to feel respected. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes, if I'm at the agency and I hear some of the junior detectives discussing a lead, or even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway... I might sort of just... jump in? You know, offer a little advice or ask a quick question before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful! I just get so enthusiastic about solving things, or I want to make sure they aren't missing a detail. I mean, afterward, I do sometimes realize, \"Oh, I just barged right into their conversation,\" and I feel a little bad about it. But I always apologize if I feel like I stepped on any toes! \n\nThat's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope people don't think I'm being pushy or intrusive. I'd hate for you to think I don't have good social skills!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself saying things that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Saying things I regret? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that very often. I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Being kind is so important, especially in my line of work when people are already stressed out.\n\nI mean, I guess every now and then, if I'm really caught up in the moment—like if a case is getting intense or I'm just super passionate about something—I might speak a little... freely? Like, maybe I'll give a piece of advice that wasn't exactly asked for, or I'll be a bit too honest about a situation. But it's never meant to be hurtful! I just want to be transparent and help people see the whole picture. \n\nIf I ever feel like something I said didn't land right, I always make sure to smooth it over right away. I would just be devastated if I actually upset someone. \n\nEveryone has moments where they could have phrased something a little better, don't they? I really do try my best to be thoughtful about what I say.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble controlling your impulses in social interactions?\n\nPatient: Trouble controlling impulses? Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I have *trouble* with it. I really value being a polite and agreeable person, you know? It's so important to me to make sure everyone feels comfortable when I'm around them. \n\nI mean, I suppose I'm just a very... enthusiastic person! Sometimes if I'm at a gathering or talking with clients, I might get a little carried away and maybe laugh a bit too loud, or I might jump up to help someone before they even ask. But that's just because I want to be involved and make sure everyone is having a good time, or getting the help they need. \n\nI really try to read the room, though. I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. When I'm by myself, I'm actually pretty quiet. I mostly just focus on my case files, or maybe catch up on some paperwork for the agency. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes I might suddenly decide to deep-clean my office or start looking into a background check I hadn't planned on doing right that second, but... well, that's just being dedicated to the job, right? It's nothing reckless or out of control. I really pride myself on being responsible, even when no one is watching. \n\nI don't really get urges to do anything strange or impulsive when I'm on my own. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! \n\nI mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? I have to notice the little things—who just walked into the room, what kind of car is idling across the street, that sort of thing. So if it ever seems like I'm looking around a lot or noticing things outside the window, it's really just me being thorough and observant. \n\nI'd never let myself get pulled away from what I'm supposed to be doing. I take my responsibilities very seriously. Being highly observant is a good trait to have, isn't it? I really try my best to stay on top of everything and do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking, even if you don't mean to?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I interrupt people. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. Being polite and respectful of others is just so important to me. \n\nI mean... I suppose sometimes I can be a little *too* eager to help? Like, if I happen to hear the junior detectives at the agency discussing a lead, or maybe even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway, I might sort of... jump in. Just to offer some advice or ask a few questions before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful and share my experience! I just get so invested in solving problems and making sure people have the right information. Though, I will admit, sometimes afterward I'll look back and realize that I kind of barged into someone else's conversation. I always feel terrible when I realize I've done that, because I'd hate for anyone to think I'm rude or overbearing. I really do mean well. That's just being a collaborative team player, right? I really hope they see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talk more than others? Oh, um... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything like that. I really try to be engaging and make sure the people I'm with feel comfortable. I think communication is just so vital, especially in my line of work.\n\nI mean, I do like to be thorough. Sometimes, if I'm discussing a case or going over a client meeting, I... well, I guess I can get a little caught up in the details. I just want to make sure I'm explaining every single angle so nothing is missed. I'll be talking through all the possibilities, and sometimes I'll realize I've sort of... filled up all the space in the conversation. Even when the other person is maybe shifting around or looking at their watch, giving me those little signs that they're ready to move on. \n\nBut I'm just trying to be comprehensive and make sure they have all the facts! It's so important to be clear, don't you think? I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just rambling or not letting them speak. I really just want to do a good job and make sure everything is understood. That's a good quality for an investigator, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone?\n\nPatient: On impulse? Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really try to be very deliberate about my choices. When I'm alone, I'm usually just... well, I'm usually catching up on paperwork or reviewing case files. I like to keep my time very structured and productive. \n\nI mean, I guess I might occasionally decide to reorganize my entire desk out of nowhere, but that's just because I want everything to be neat and ready for the next day! I wouldn't call that impulsive, would you? I just think it's so important to stay organized and not make careless decisions. I really try my hardest to be responsible and think things through. Being methodical like that is a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I fidget. I really try to sit nicely and maintain a professional posture. It's so important to look put-together, especially when I'm working on a case. \n\nI mean... I suppose when I'm stuck at the computer for hours, just combing through endless public records or financial statements, I might... well, I guess my foot might start bouncing under the desk a little bit. Or I might find myself clicking a pen without really thinking about it. And sometimes I do shift around in my chair, maybe a bit more than I should, to the point where I actually lose my place in what I'm reading. \n\nBut it's just because I'm trying to stay alert and focused on the task! Those records can be so dry, and I just want to make sure I'm keeping my energy up so I don't miss any important details for the client. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just restless or not taking the work seriously. I really do try to stay on task. That's just a normal way to keep the mind sharp during tedious work, isn't it? I really hope it doesn't sound like a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're speaking without fully thinking through what you're going to say?\n\nPatient: Speaking without thinking? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that. I really pride myself on being thoughtful and professional when I speak. In my line of work, you have to be very careful about what you say, especially when interviewing witnesses or talking to clients. I always try to be very intentional.\n\nI mean... I guess there are times when I'm really excited about a lead, or I've just figured out a connection in a case, and I might... well, I might just blurt it out before I've perfectly crafted the sentence. Sometimes the thoughts are just moving so fast, and I want to get the information out there quickly so we can act on it! \n\nBut it's just because I'm so passionate about getting to the bottom of things and helping my clients. I'm not trying to be careless or rude, I promise. I just get very invested in the work. It's actually a good thing to be enthusiastic and quick on your feet like that, don't you think? I'd hate for it to seem like I'm just blurting things out for no reason. I really do try my best to be professional.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself making careless mistakes in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Careless mistakes? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really pride myself on being very precise. When you're an investigator, you just have to be. I try so hard to make sure every single detail in my reports is perfect. \n\nI mean... I guess occasionally I might, I don't know, misspell a word in my rough notes when I'm writing really fast, but I always run a spell-check and review everything before anyone else sees it! I would never let a real mistake slip through to a client. I just care too much about doing a good job and being completely thorough. \n\nIt's so important to be reliable like that, don't you agree? I'd be absolutely mortified if I handed in something with errors. I really hope I don't seem like someone who doesn't pay attention to the details, because I truly do try my absolute best to get everything exactly right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often lose things that are necessary for tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Lose things? Oh, no, definitely not. I really pride myself on being very organized. As an investigator, you simply have to know exactly where your tools are—your voice recorder, your camera, the case files... I would never just lose them. I keep a very strict system for all of my belongings.\n\nI mean... I suppose if you count, like, leaving a coffee mug in the car, or maybe misplacing a chapstick every now and then, I might do that occasionally. Just normal little things when I'm rushing out the door to meet a client. But nothing actually necessary for my tasks! I have a very specific place for all my work materials and my keys, and I always put them right back where they belong the second I'm done with them. \n\nIt's just a matter of being responsible, isn't it? I would be absolutely mortified if I couldn't find an important document when someone asked for it. I truly make sure I have everything under control and accounted for. Being dependable like that is just part of being a professional, don't you think? I really wouldn't want you to think I'm disorganized or careless with my things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's so important to be clear, don't you think? I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just rambling or not letting them speak. I really just want to do a good job and make sure everything is understood. That's a good quality for an investigator, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone?\n\nPatient: On impulse? Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really try to be very deliberate about my choices. When I'm alone, I'm usually just... well, I'm usually catching up on paperwork or reviewing case files. I like to keep my time very structured and productive. \n\nI mean, I guess I might occasionally decide to reorganize my entire desk out of nowhere, but that's just because I want everything to be neat and ready for the next day! I wouldn't call that impulsive, would you? I just think it's so important to stay organized and not make careless decisions. I really try my hardest to be responsible and think things through. Being methodical like that is a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I fidget. I really try to sit nicely and maintain a professional posture. It's so important to look put-together, especially when I'm working on a case. \n\nI mean... I suppose when I'm stuck at the computer for hours, just combing through endless public records or financial statements, I might... well, I guess my foot might start bouncing under the desk a little bit. Those records can be so dry, and I just want to make sure I'm keeping my energy up so I don't miss any important details for the client. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just restless or not taking the work seriously. I really do try to stay on task. That's just a normal way to keep the mind sharp during tedious work, isn't it? Dominating conversations? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use that word at all. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. As an investigator, listening is such a huge part of the job, and I always want my clients and colleagues to feel heard and valued.

I'd hate to be one of those people who just does whatever they want without thinking of others. I always try to be mindful. That's the right way to be, isn't it? I really hope I come across as a considerate person.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting on impulses when you're alone?\n\nPatient: When I'm alone? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. When I'm by myself, I'm actually pretty quiet. I mostly just focus on my case files, or maybe catch up on some paperwork for the agency. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes I might suddenly decide to deep-clean my office or start looking into a background check I hadn't planned on doing right that second, but... well, that's just being dedicated to the job, right? It's nothing reckless or out of control. I really pride myself on being responsible, even when no one is watching. \n\nI don't really get urges to do anything strange or impulsive when I'm on my own. I like to keep things orderly. That sounds like a pretty normal, healthy way to be, doesn't it? I'd hate for you to think I'm chaotic behind closed doors!\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Distracted? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't use the word distracted. I think I'm actually very focused! \n\nI mean, in my line of work as an investigator, paying attention to my surroundings is just... well, it's part of the job, you know? I have to notice the little things—who just walked into the room, what kind of car is idling across the street, that sort of thing. So if it ever seems like I'm looking around a lot or noticing things outside the window, it's really just me being thorough and observant. \n\nI'd never let myself get pulled away from what I'm supposed to be doing. I take my responsibilities very seriously. Being highly observant is a good trait to have, isn't it? I really try my best to stay on top of everything and do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking, even if you don't mean to?\n\nPatient: Interrupting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I interrupt people. I really try my hardest to be a good listener. Being polite and respectful of others is just so important to me. \n\nI mean... I suppose sometimes I can be a little *too* eager to help? Like, if I happen to hear the junior detectives at the agency discussing a lead, or maybe even just a neighbor talking out in the hallway, I might sort of... jump in. Just to offer some advice or ask a few questions before they've actually invited me into the chat. \n\nBut it's only because I want to be helpful and share my experience! I just get so invested in solving problems and making sure people have the right information. Though, I will admit, sometimes afterward I'll look back and realize that I kind of barged into someone else's conversation. I always feel terrible when I realize I've done that, because I'd hate for anyone to think I'm rude or overbearing. I really do mean well. That's just being a collaborative team player, right? I really hope they see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talk more than others? Oh, um... I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything like that. I really try to be engaging and make sure the people I'm with feel comfortable. I think communication is just so vital, especially in my line of work.\n\nI mean, I do like to be thorough. Sometimes, if I'm discussing a case or going over a client meeting, I... well, I guess I can get a little caught up in the details. I just want to make sure I'm explaining every single angle so nothing is missed. I'll be talking through all the possibilities, and sometimes I'll realize I've sort of... filled up all the space in the conversation. Even when the other person is maybe shifting around or looking at their watch, giving me those little signs that they're ready to move on. \n\nBut I'm just trying to be comprehensive and make sure they have all the facts! It's so important to be clear, don't you think? I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just rambling or not letting them speak. I really just want to do a good job and make sure everything is understood. That's a good quality for an investigator, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone?\n\nPatient: On impulse? Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really try to be very deliberate about my choices. When I'm alone, I'm usually just... well, I'm usually catching up on paperwork or reviewing case files. I like to keep my time very structured and productive. \n\nI mean, I guess I might occasionally decide to reorganize my entire desk out of nowhere, but that's just because I want everything to be neat and ready for the next day! I wouldn't call that impulsive, would you? I just think it's so important to stay organized and not make careless decisions. I really try my hardest to be responsible and think things through. Being methodical like that is a good thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I fidget. I really try to sit nicely and maintain a professional posture. It's so important to look put-together, especially when I'm working on a case. \n\nI mean... I suppose when I'm stuck at the computer for hours, just combing through endless public records or financial statements, I might... well, I guess my foot might start bouncing under the desk a little bit. Or I might find myself clicking a pen without really thinking about it. And sometimes I do shift around in my chair, maybe a bit more than I should, to the point where I actually lose my place in what I'm reading. \n\nBut it's just because I'm trying to stay alert and focused on the task! Those records can be so dry, and I just want to make sure I'm keeping my energy up so I don't miss any important details for the client. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just restless or not taking the work seriously. I really do try to stay on task. That's just a normal way to keep the mind sharp during tedious work, isn't it? I really hope it doesn't sound like a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're speaking without fully thinking through what you're going to say?\n\nPatient: Speaking without thinking? Oh, um... no, I don't think I do that. I really pride myself on being thoughtful and professional when I speak. In my line of work, you have to be very careful about what you say, especially when interviewing witnesses or talking to clients. I always try to be very intentional.\n\nI mean... I guess there are times when I'm really excited about a lead, or I've just figured out a connection in a case, and I might... well, I might just blurt it out before I've perfectly crafted the sentence. Sometimes the thoughts are just moving so fast, and I want to get the information out there quickly so we can act on it! \n\nBut it's just because I'm so passionate about getting to the bottom of things and helping my clients. I'm not trying to be careless or rude, I promise. I just get very invested in the work. It's actually a good thing to be enthusiastic and quick on your feet like that, don't you think? I'd hate for it to seem like I'm just blurting things out for no reason. I really do try my best to be professional.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself making careless mistakes in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Careless mistakes? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really pride myself on being very precise. When you're an investigator, you just have to be. I try so hard to make sure every single detail in my reports is perfect. \n\nI mean... I guess occasionally I might, I don't know, misspell a word in my rough notes when I'm writing really fast, but I always run a spell-check and review everything before anyone else sees it! I would never let a real mistake slip through to a client. I just care too much about doing a good job and being completely thorough. \n\nIt's so important to be reliable like that, don't you agree? I'd be absolutely mortified if I handed in something with errors. I really hope I don't seem like someone who doesn't pay attention to the details, because I truly do try my absolute best to get everything exactly right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often lose things that are necessary for tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Lose things? I would be absolutely mortified if I couldn't find an important document when someone asked for it. I truly make sure I have everything under control and accounted for. Being dependable like that is just part of being a professional, don't you think? I really wouldn't want you to think I'm disorganized or careless with my things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations to the point where others have difficulty contributing?\n\nPatient: Dominating conversations? And I guess sometimes I'll realize I've basically filled all the space in the room... even when the other person is giving me clear signs they're done and ready to wrap up. \n\nBut it's truly just because I want to give them the absolute best service and make sure they understand all the facts before we move forward. I'm definitely not trying to be overbearing or talk over anyone. It's just a sign of being really dedicated and thorough with the work, don't you think? I'd hate for anyone to think I don't care about what they have to say. I really do try to be polite and professional in all my meetings."
